\displaystyle(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\sigma_{0}k^{\prime}_{0}(w^{\prime}_{0}+z^{\prime}_{0}).

Taking
\partial_{\lambda}
of these equations gives

(6.2)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{w}_{0}

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{0}-\tfrac{1}{4\gamma}\widetilde{w}_{0}k^{\prime}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{4\gamma}\widetilde{w}_{0}k^{\prime}_{0},

\displaystyle\mathring{K}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{t}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\widetilde{w}_{0}z^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\widetilde{w}^{\prime}_{0},

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}[\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{w}_{0}k^{\prime}_{0}(w^{\prime}_{0}+z^{\prime}_{0})+\sigma_{0}k^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{w}_{0}^{\prime}],

at time
t=0
. Now take
p^{m}_{x}
of these equations for
m=0,\ldots,2n
and perform computations analogous to those in the
\beta_{t}=0
step of the proof of Proposition
5.1
to conclude our base case. One can also compute the analog of (
5.2
),

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\partial^{m}_{x}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!L\mathcal{C}^{m}_{x}}\leq 1+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{4\gamma},

to use in the inductive step.

Inductive Step:
The inductive step will also be analogous to the inductive step in the proof of Proposition
5.1
: fix
0\leq m\leq 2n-1
, and suppose our result is true for all multi indices
\beta
with
|\beta|\leq 2n
and
\beta_{t}\leq m
.

We first do the inductive step for
\mathring{K}_{\lambda}
and
\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}
. Let
|\beta|\leq 2n-1,\beta_{t}=m
. Take the identities for
\partial^{\beta+e_{t}}\mathring{K}
and
\partial^{\beta+e_{t}}\mathring{Z}
used in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, apply
\partial_{\lambda}
to each of these equations, and use (
6.2
) to simplify. From here, use the inductive hypotheses and (
5.1
) to obtain bounds on
\partial^{\beta+e_{t}}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}
and
\partial^{\beta+e_{t}}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}
via the same types of computations as used in the proof of Proposition
5.1
. Because
\mathcal{C}_{x}\gg 1,\mathcal{C}_{t}\gg\alpha\mathcal{C}_{x},
and
\mathcal{C}_{t}\gg 1+\alpha
, the computations go through just like before.

The inductive step for the remaining three inequalities can be done using (
5.1
) and our inductive hypotheses in conjunction with our bounds on
\partial^{\beta}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}
and
\partial^{\beta}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}
in a manner completely analogous to the corresponding steps in the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

6.2.
A Priori estimates for time derivatives

In this section, we will fix a time
T\in(0,T_{*}]
, a positive function
C_{t}:[0,T_{*})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, a constant
\delta\geq 0
, and constants
A,M
satisfying

A\geq 10B_{z},\qquad M\geq\max(1,5L),

and assume that

(6.3)

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq A\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq A\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n.
We will furthermore assume that

(6.4)

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\leq B_{k}\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\leq B_{z}\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n+1.
Note that the hypotheses (
6.4
) imply that the a priori estimates of §
5.2
all hold with
A_{k}=B_{k},A_{z}=B_{z}
for these choices of
C_{t}
and
\delta
. We will use this fact throughout this subsection and the next.

Proposition 6.2
.

If
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
and
A\varepsilon\ll 1
, then for all
t\in[0,T]
we have

(6.5)

\frac{\|\Sigma_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{M}\leq\tfrac{1}{9},\qquad\frac{\|\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{M}\leq 1,\qquad\frac{\|(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{M}\leq\tfrac{3}{4}.

Proof.

(
6.1a
) gives us a Duhamel formula for
\Sigma_{\lambda}
from which it follows that

\displaystyle|\Sigma_{\lambda}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|\widetilde{w}_{0}(x)|e^{\mathcal{O}(\alpha tB_{z}\varepsilon)}+\alpha tMA\varepsilon[3+\tfrac{9}{2\gamma}]

for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}\times[0,T]
. Since
M\geq\max(1,5L)
, if
B_{z}\varepsilon
and
A\varepsilon
are small enough we have
\|\Sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\frac{M}{9}
.

The remaining bounds follow from a bootstrap argument. Let
T^{\prime}\in[0,T]
and assume that

\frac{\|\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{M}\leq 2,\qquad\frac{\|(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{M}\leq\tfrac{3}{4}+\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
. Then using (
6.1b
), (
6.1c
), and the equations

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}

\displaystyle=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\>ds,

\displaystyle(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{w}_{0}^{\prime}-\tfrac{1}{4\gamma}\widetilde{w}_{0}k^{\prime}_{0}+\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{\lambda}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}\>ds

allows us to prove (
6.5
) for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
, which concludes our bootstrap argument.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

If the hypotheses of the previous proposition are satisfied, and
C_{t}(0),\varepsilon
also satisfy

(1+\alpha)3^{\delta}\ll C_{t}(0),\qquad(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1,

then

(6.6)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 8\alpha A\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(\delta_{0m}+9A\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq A\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],0\leq m\leq 2n
.

Proof.

The proof of these estimates is analogous to the proof of Proposition
5.2
: do induction on
m
; at each step, take
\partial^{m}_{t}
of the identities (
6.1
), apply (
6.3
), the inductive hypotheses, and the estimates from §
5.2
; then use the bounds from §
B
and the fact that
\frac{(1+\alpha)3^{\delta}}{C_{t}}
is sufficiently small to conclude.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Under the hypotheses of the previous proposition, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 1,

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{3}{4},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 1,

for all
t\in[0,T],0\leq m\leq 2n
and

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m-1}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq\tfrac{10}{\alpha}A\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m-1}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq\tfrac{5}{\alpha}A\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],\>1\leq m\leq 2n
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
6.3
the same way that Corollary
5.3
follows from Proposition
5.2
.
∎

6.3.
Energy estimates for time derivatives

Proposition 6.5
.

Define the constant

(6.7)

B_{\lambda}:=\tfrac{5}{2}\cdot 9^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\cdot e^{31}.

Let
\delta,\kappa
be as in (
5.10
), and let
C_{t}
and
\overline{C}_{t}
be defined as in (
5.11
) and (
5.27
) with these values of
\delta,\kappa
. If
C_{t}(0)
and
M
satisfy

M\geq 5L,\qquad C_{t}(0)\gg(1+\alpha)3^{\delta},\qquad C_{t}(0)\gg\alpha C_{0}

and
\varepsilon
satisfies

\displaystyle(1+\alpha)^{2}B_{z}^{2}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\ll 1,

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\ll 1,

then

(6.8)

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T_{*}),\>0\leq m\leq 2n
.

Proof.

The proof of these estimates is analogous to the energy estimates in the proofs of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.4
. We will detail the keys steps of the proof and highlight the slight differences with the previous energy estimates. We proceed as before with a bootstrap argument. Pick a time
T\in[0,T_{*})
and constant
A
satisfying

B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}<A<\tfrac{4}{3}B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z},

and assume that

\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq A\varepsilon,\qquad\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\leq A\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n
. Since
\delta
and
\kappa
satisfy (
5.10
), if
C_{t}(0)
is chosen large enough and
\varepsilon
is chosen small enough such that they satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition
5.4
, we can conclude that (
6.4
) holds. Suppose that
B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough that the hypotheses of Proposition
6.2
are satisfied with our choice of
A
. If
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough, these bootstrap hypotheses now imply that we can apply the a priori estimates of §
6.2
to our solution with our chosen values of
A
and
M
. Just like in the proof of Proposition
5.4
, our hypotheses on
C_{t}(0),M
, and
\varepsilon
allow us to apply Proposition
6.1
with
\mathcal{C}_{t}:=\frac{1}{2}2^{-\delta}C_{t}(0)
and get

(6.9)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC_{t}(0)^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{5}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2^{m}},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC_{t}(0)^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{5}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2^{m}},

at time
t=0
for all
0\leq m\leq 2n
.

For
m=0,\ldots,2n
define the energies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-q\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\eta_{x}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\>dx&m=0\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\delta qm}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\>dx&1\leq m\leq 2n\end{cases},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-q\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\>dx&m=0\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\delta qm}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\>dx&1\leq m\leq 2n\end{cases},

and for
1\leq m\leq 2n
define the energies

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(m+1)^{2q}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}}{(m!)^{q}M^{q}C^{mq}_{t}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(m+1)^{2q}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}}{(m!)^{q}M^{q}C^{mq}_{t}}.

We will prove (
6.8
) for
t\in[0,T]
separately in the case
m=0
and the case
1\leq m\leq 2n
, and since this strictly improves upon our bootstrap assumptions our bootstrap argument will be complete.

Case 1 (
m=0
):
We will use our bootstrap hypotheses and the estimates from §
5
to perform energy estimates on
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z}
similar to those in the proof of Proposition
4.1
. Since the parameter
\delta
does not appear in
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z}
, let us abuse notation and define
\delta:=\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}
just for the
m=0
case.

Just like in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, taking the time derivative of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}
and using our identities (
6.1
) gives us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{k}

\displaystyle=[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\delta q-\tfrac{q-1}{2}]\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}+[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\delta q-\tfrac{q-1}{2}]\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle+q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{K}_{\lambda})[-\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\partial_{t}\mathring{K}+\alpha\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma\partial_{x}\mathring{K}]

\displaystyle-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{x}\mathring{K}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}|\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\tfrac{1}{2}\mathring{K}[(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}+\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}],

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{z}

\displaystyle=[\alpha\delta q-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}(q-1)]\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}

\displaystyle+\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\big[-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(q-1)\mathring{Z}+[\alpha\delta q+\alpha(\tfrac{q}{2}-1)]\tfrac{1}{2\gamma}\Sigma\mathring{K}]

\displaystyle+q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})[-\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\partial_{t}\mathring{Z}+2\alpha\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}]

\displaystyle+q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\Sigma(\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}-\eta_{x}\mathring{W})

\displaystyle+q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\mathring{Z}[-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}]

\displaystyle+q\int\Sigma^{-\delta q}\eta_{x}|\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\mathring{K}\mathring{Z}(\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\Sigma+\eta_{x}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma).

The bounds (
5.7
) and (
5.29
) tell us that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{5}{2\alpha}\overline{C}_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{5}{4\alpha}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\overline{C}_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\mathring{Z}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon.

Therefore, applying (
4.8
) as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
gives us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{k}+\bigg[\tfrac{1}{2}[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\delta q-\tfrac{q-1}{2}]-(q-1)(\tfrac{B_{k}}{B_{z}}+\tfrac{3}{2}B_{k}\varepsilon)\bigg]\|\Sigma^{-\delta}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{x}}-\tfrac{3}{2}B_{k}\varepsilon\|\Sigma^{-\delta}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq(4+B_{z}\varepsilon)[\tfrac{\alpha}{2}\delta q-\tfrac{q-1}{2}]\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}+\big(3^{\delta}MB_{z}(\tfrac{19}{36}\overline{C}_{t}+\tfrac{3}{8}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{z}\varepsilon)\varepsilon\big)^{q},

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{z}-\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma}(1+\tfrac{9}{4}B_{z}\varepsilon)\|\Sigma^{-\delta}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{x}}+\bigg[\tfrac{1}{2}[\alpha\delta q-\tfrac{q-1}{2}]-(q-1)[\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma}(1+\tfrac{9}{4}B_{z}\varepsilon)+\tfrac{1}{2}]\bigg]\|\Sigma^{-\delta}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(4[\alpha\delta q-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}(q-1)]+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(q-1)B_{z}\varepsilon+\tfrac{3}{2\gamma}[\alpha\delta q+\alpha(\tfrac{q}{2}-1)]B_{k}\varepsilon\big)\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z}

\displaystyle+\bigg(2\cdot 3^{\delta}MB_{z}(\tfrac{19}{36}\overline{C}_{t}+\tfrac{3|1-\alpha|}{8}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}B_{z}\varepsilon+\tfrac{5\alpha}{6\gamma}B_{k}\varepsilon)\varepsilon\bigg)^{q}.

Since
\overline{C}_{t}>e^{5}C_{t}(0)\gg 1+\alpha
, it follows that if
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough then

\displaystyle\tfrac{19}{36}\overline{C}_{t}+\tfrac{3}{8}+\tfrac{1}{2}B_{z}\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\tfrac{2}{3}\overline{C}_{t},

\displaystyle 2(\tfrac{19}{36}\overline{C}_{t}+\tfrac{3|1-\alpha|}{8}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}B_{z}\varepsilon+\tfrac{5\alpha}{6\gamma}B_{k}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle<\tfrac{4}{3}\overline{C}_{t}.

Adding our inequalities together and using our choice
\delta=\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}
implies that

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{k}+\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{z}

\displaystyle\leq 15\max(1,\alpha)q(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z})+(\tfrac{2}{3}3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}M\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon)^{q}+(\tfrac{4}{3}3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}M\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon)^{q}.

ODE comparison now gives us

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}(t)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\bigg[(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}(0)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z}(0))+\frac{(\tfrac{2}{3}3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}M\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon)^{q}+(\tfrac{4}{3}3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}M\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon)^{q}}{15\max(1,\alpha)q}\bigg]e^{15\max(1,\alpha)qt}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Taking
q
th roots of both sides, sending
q\rightarrow\infty
, and using (
6.9
) and (
4.12a
) now produces

\displaystyle\max(\|\Sigma^{-\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\|\Sigma^{-\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\leq M\varepsilon\bigg[\tfrac{2^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}}{5}+2\cdot 3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\bigg]e^{31}

for all
t\in[0,T]
. This gives us (
6.8
) for
m=0
,
t\in[0,T]
.

Case 2 (
1\leq m\leq 2n
):
Let
m\geq 1
. If
D_{k}=D_{k}(m,q,\alpha)
is the same constant defined by (
5.16
), then the same computations as in the proof of Proposition
5.4
give us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle=D_{k}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}+(D_{k}+\alpha mq)\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}mq\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q}\Sigma\mathring{K}-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\partial^{m+1}_{t}\mathring{K}

\displaystyle+q\alpha\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\Sigma_{\lambda}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}

\displaystyle-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}[\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}+m\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}]

\displaystyle-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}[\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle-q\sum^{m}_{j=2}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})[\partial^{j-1}_{t}\eta_{xt}\partial^{m+1-j}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}+\partial^{j-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\partial^{m+1-j}_{t}\mathring{K}]

\displaystyle+\alpha q\sum^{m}_{j=1}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})[\partial^{j-1}_{t}\Sigma_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}+\partial^{j-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}]

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}q\sum^{m}_{j=1}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})[\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\partial^{j}_{t}\mathring{Z}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\mathring{K}+\eta_{x}\partial^{j}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\mathring{K}+\eta_{x}\partial^{j}_{t}\mathring{Z}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}q\sum^{m}_{j=1}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})[\partial^{j-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\mathring{K}+\partial^{j-1}_{t}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=m\\
j_{1}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{m\choose{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial^{j_{1}-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\partial^{j_{2}}_{t}\mathring{K}\partial^{j_{3}}_{t}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=m\\
j_{1}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{m\choose{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial^{j_{1}-1}_{t}\eta_{xt}\partial^{j_{2}}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\partial^{j_{3}}_{t}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=m\\
j_{1}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{m\choose{j_{1}j_{2}j_{3}}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda})\partial^{j_{1}-1}_{t}\eta_{xt}\partial^{j_{2}}_{t}\mathring{K}\partial^{j_{3}}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}.

Just like in the proof of Proposition
5.4
, applying (
4.8
) to the first term of the righthand side, multiplying through by
(\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}})^{q}
, adding
-\kappa mq\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}
to both sides, and using the formulas from §
B
gives us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\tfrac{1}{2}D_{k}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

\displaystyle\leq[-mq\kappa+(4+B_{z}\varepsilon)D_{k}+\alpha mq(B_{z}+\tfrac{3}{2\gamma}B_{k}))\varepsilon]\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m+1}_{t}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{\alpha}{9}q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}(\tfrac{3}{8}+m\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2})B_{k}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\tfrac{15}{2}(1+\alpha)AB_{z}\varepsilon^{2}\sum^{m}_{j=2}\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}(m+1-j)}{j^{3}(m+2-j)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}40\alpha(1+2\tfrac{B_{k}}{B_{z}})AB_{z}\varepsilon^{2}\sum^{m}_{j=1}\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}(m+1-j)}{j^{3}(m+2-j)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)AB_{z}\varepsilon^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)B^{2}_{z}\varepsilon^{2}).

Since
m\leq 2n
, Corollary
5.3
and (
5.28
) imply

(6.11)

\displaystyle\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{10}{\alpha}\tfrac{(m+1)^{3}}{(m+2)^{2}}3^{\delta}C_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon\leq\tfrac{10}{\alpha}(m+1)\overline{C}_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m+1}_{t}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(m+1)^{3}}{(m+2)^{2}}3^{\delta}C_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon\leq(m+1)\overline{C}_{t}B_{k}\varepsilon.

Therefore, using (
5.20
) and the bounds from §
B
yields

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\tfrac{1}{2}D_{k}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

\displaystyle\leq[-mq\kappa+(4+B_{z}\varepsilon)D_{k}+\alpha mq(B_{z}+\tfrac{3}{2\gamma}B_{k}))\varepsilon]\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+(m+1)q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}[\tfrac{19}{9}\overline{C}_{t}+\tfrac{3}{8(m+1)}+\tfrac{m}{m+1}\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(1+9A\varepsilon)]B_{k}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+mq\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}(1+\alpha)AB_{z}\varepsilon^{2}(75+440(1+2\tfrac{B_{k}}{B_{z}})\tfrac{\alpha}{1+\alpha})

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)AB_{z}\varepsilon^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)B^{2}_{z}\varepsilon^{2}).

Since
A>B_{\lambda}B_{z}\overline{C}_{t}
and
B_{\lambda}>e^{31}
, it follows that if
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we have

[\tfrac{19}{9}\overline{C}_{t}+\frac{3}{8(m+1)}+\tfrac{m}{m+1}\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(1+9A\varepsilon)]B_{z}<[\tfrac{19}{9}\overline{C}_{t}+\frac{3}{16}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}]B_{z}+\tfrac{1}{200}A<\frac{1}{100}A.

Therefore if
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we have

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\tfrac{1}{2}D_{k}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

\displaystyle\leq[-mq\kappa+(4+B_{z}\varepsilon)D_{k}+\alpha mq(B_{z}+\tfrac{3}{2\gamma}B_{k}))\varepsilon]\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+(m+1)q\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\tfrac{1}{100}A.

It now follows from the upper bound (
5.17
) on
D_{k}
and the definition of
\kappa
that if
B_{z}\varepsilon
is sufficiently small we get

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\bigg[\tfrac{1}{2}D_{k}-\tfrac{1}{10}(q-1)(m+1)]\bigg]\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\leq-mq\max(1,\alpha)\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}+(m+1)\big(\tfrac{A}{10}\big)^{q}.

Analogous computations for
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}
give us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle=D_{z}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}

\displaystyle+[\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}-q(\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}m+1+\alpha)]\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}[-1+q(\tfrac{1}{2}+m(\delta-1)]\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q}\Sigma\mathring{K}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\Sigma(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle-q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\partial^{m+1}_{t}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle+2\alpha q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\Sigma_{\lambda}\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-mq\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}[\Sigma_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}+\Sigma(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}q\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-q\sum^{m}_{j=2}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})[\partial^{j-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}\partial^{m+1-j}_{t}\mathring{Z}+\partial^{j-1}_{t}\eta_{xt}\partial^{m+1-j}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle+2\alpha q\sum^{m}_{j=2}{m\choose{j}}\int\Sigma^{-\delta mq}|\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}|^{q-1}\mathrm{sgn\,}(\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda})[\partial^{j-1}_{t}\Sigma_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}+\partial^{j-1}_{t}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{t}\partial^{m-j}_{t}\partial_{x}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}]

\displaystyle+(\ldots)

where
D_{z}
is the same constant defined in (
5.25
) and the remaining terms referred to as “
\ldots
” are bounded via

\bigg[\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}|(\ldots)|\leq q\bigg[\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q-1}\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)(A+B_{z})B_{z}\varepsilon^{2}).

Therefore, performing the same types of computations done for
\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}
gives us

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{z}-\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma}\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg[\tfrac{1}{2}D_{z}-(q-1)[(m+1)(\tfrac{1}{10}+\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma})\bigg]\bigg[\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg]^{q}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\leq-mq\max(1,\alpha)\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{z}+(m+1)(\tfrac{1}{10}A\varepsilon)^{q}.

Using our bounds (
5.17
) and (
5.26
), we get

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}D_{k}

\displaystyle>\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma}+\tfrac{1}{10}(q-1)(m+1),

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}D_{z}

\displaystyle>(q-1)(m+1)(\tfrac{1}{10}+\tfrac{\alpha}{\gamma}),

so we can add (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) together to arrive at

\displaystyle\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\dot{\widetilde{E}}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle\leq-mq\max(1,\alpha)(\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{k}+\widetilde{E}^{m,q}_{z})+2(m+1)(\tfrac{1}{10}A\varepsilon)^{q}.

Using ODE comparison (see Lemma
B.1
), raising both sides to the
\frac{1}{q}
, applying (
6.9
), and sending
q\rightarrow\infty
gives us

\displaystyle\max\bigg(\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}},\tfrac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!MC^{m}_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\max\bigg(\tfrac{1}{5}\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2^{m}},\tfrac{1}{10}A\varepsilon\bigg)=\tfrac{1}{10}A\varepsilon.

Since
A<\frac{4}{3}\overline{C}_{t}B_{\lambda}B_{z}
, we conclude that (
6.8
) is true for
t\in[0,T]
. This closes our bootstrap argument.∎

6.4.
Bounds on the remaining derivatives

The results of this section are all analogs of the results of §
5.4
. Their proofs are also completely analogous. For this reason, they will be omitted.

Proposition 6.6
.

Suppose the hypotheses of Proposition
6.5
are satisfied, and additionally
\overline{C}_{x}
is a constant satisfying

\overline{C}_{x}\gg 1,\qquad\alpha\overline{C}_{x}\gg\overline{C}_{t}.

Under these conditions, if
\varepsilon
is small enough such that

(1+\alpha)B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1,

then the following bounds hold for each
t\in[0,T_{*})
and each
\beta
with
|\beta|\leq 2n
:

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\mathring{K}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\mathring{Z}_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 8\alpha B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 1,

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq\tfrac{3}{4},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\lambda}\Sigma_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 1.

Corollary 6.7
.

Under the hypotheses of the previous proposition, we also have that

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\Sigma_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{9},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\lambda}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial^{\beta}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})_{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!M\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 185\max(1,\alpha)B_{\lambda}\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon.

for all
t\in[0,T_{*})
and
\beta
with
|\beta|\leq 2n
.

Corollary 6.8
.

Under the hypotheses of the previous propositions, we have that

(6.14a)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{xt}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial^{i+1}_{x}\widetilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 190\max(1,\alpha)\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}M\overline{C}^{i}_{x}\overline{C}_{t}B_{\lambda}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(6.14b)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\partial_{\lambda}\eta_{x}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t\partial^{i+1}_{x}\widetilde{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 190\max(1,\alpha)t\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}M\overline{C}^{i}_{x}\overline{C}_{t}B_{\lambda}B_{z}\varepsilon

for all
t\in[0,T_{*}),i=0,\ldots,2n
.

7.
Solution continuity with respect to initial data

In this section, we will show that solutions with initial data in
\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
depend continuously on their initial data with respect to the
(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
topology. For
m=0,\ldots,2n+1
define the seminorm
10
10

10

It is a seminorm because it doesn’t detect constant changes in
k_{0}
; however our system (
3.2
) does not detect constant changes in
k_{0}
either.
on
(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}

(7.1)

\|(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\|_{m,C_{0}}:=\max_{0\leq i\leq m}\bigg(\|w_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\|z_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}w_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!C^{i}_{0}},\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}z_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!C^{i}_{0}},\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}k_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!C^{i}_{0}}\bigg).

Note that
\|(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\|_{m,C_{0}}\leq\|(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\|_{(W^{m+1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}}
. We will estimate the difference of two solutions in terms of the distance between their initial data with respect to these seminorms.

The methods and results of this section will be completely analogous to those in the previous two sections. For this reason, we will only provide a sketch of the proofs.

For the rest of this section, fix
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0}),(\widetilde{w}_{0},\widetilde{z}_{0},\widetilde{k}_{0})\in\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
and let the corresponding solutions of (
4.5
) with initial data
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})
and
(\widetilde{w}_{0},\widetilde{z}_{0},\widetilde{k}_{0})
be
\mathring{K},\mathring{Z},\eta_{x},\Sigma,\eta_{x}\mathring{W}
and
\widetilde{\mathring{K}},\widetilde{\mathring{Z}},\widetilde{\eta}_{x},\widetilde{\Sigma},\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}}
respectively. Let their respective blowup times be
T_{*}
and
\widetilde{T}_{*}
. Adopt the notation

\displaystyle\mu_{m}

\displaystyle:=\|(w_{0}-\widetilde{w}_{0},z_{0}-\widetilde{z}_{0},k_{0}-\widetilde{k}_{0})\|_{m,C_{0}}

\displaystyle m=0,\ldots,2n.

It follows from (
4.5
) that the difference of the two solutions satisfies

(7.2a)

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{t}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})[-\mathring{Z}+\tfrac{1}{2\gamma}(\Sigma+\widetilde{\Sigma})\mathring{K}]-\alpha(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\widetilde{\Sigma}+\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{2}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}}),

(7.2b)

\displaystyle\eta_{xt}-\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}[(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})-(\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})]+\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\mathring{Z}+\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma\mathring{K}+\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\mathring{K}+\tfrac{\alpha}{2\gamma}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}}),

(7.2c)

\displaystyle(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})_{t}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\mathring{K}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}+\eta_{x}\mathring{Z})+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}+\eta_{x}\mathring{Z})

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\mathring{Z}+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}),

(7.2d)

\displaystyle(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{x}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})-\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\mathring{Z}-\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2\gamma}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma\mathring{K}+\tfrac{1}{2\gamma}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\mathring{K}+\tfrac{1}{2\gamma}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}}).

We will also replace (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) with the identities

(7.3a)

\displaystyle\eta_{x}\partial_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})

\displaystyle=\alpha\Sigma\partial_{x}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})-\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})-\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{x}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle-(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}+\alpha(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\partial_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})-\tfrac{1}{2}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\mathring{Z}-\tfrac{1}{2}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}),

(7.3b)

\displaystyle\eta_{x}\partial_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle=2\alpha\Sigma\partial_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\eta_{x}\Sigma\mathring{K}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle-(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}+2\alpha(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\partial_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\mathring{K}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\eta_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\eta_{x}\Sigma(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\eta_{x}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}+\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}.

Multiplying these identities by
\Sigma^{-1}
and rearranging gives us

(7.4a)

\displaystyle\alpha\partial_{x}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})

\displaystyle=\eta_{x}\Sigma^{-1}\partial_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})+(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma^{-1}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}-\alpha\Sigma^{-1}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\partial_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\Sigma^{-1}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})(\eta_{x}\mathring{W})+\tfrac{1}{2}\eta_{x}\Sigma^{-1}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\mathring{Z}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})+\tfrac{1}{2}\Sigma^{-1}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\mathring{Z}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}),

(7.4b)

\displaystyle 2\alpha\partial_{x}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle=\eta_{x}\Sigma^{-1}\partial_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})+(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma^{-1}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}-2\alpha\Sigma^{-1}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\partial_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}\Sigma^{-1}\eta_{x}\mathring{W}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\Sigma^{-1}\eta_{x}\mathring{Z}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\Sigma^{-1}\eta_{x}\Sigma\mathring{K}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1-\alpha}{2}\Sigma^{-1}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\eta_{x}\Sigma^{-1}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\eta_{x}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}\eta_{x}\Sigma^{-1}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}-\tfrac{\alpha}{4\gamma}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\Sigma^{-1}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}.

Notice that the system above doesn’t feature any terms with the difference
\Sigma^{-1}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-1}
, so we do not need equations for
(\Sigma^{-1}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-1})_{t}
or
(\Sigma^{-1}-\widetilde{\Sigma}^{-1})_{x}
.

If the constants
\mathcal{C}_{x},\mathcal{C}_{t}
, and
\varepsilon
satisfy the same hypotheses as in Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
, one can prove an analog of these propositions for the difference of our two solutions. In particular, at time
t=0
one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\mathcal{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\mathcal{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\mathcal{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\mathcal{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mu_{|\beta|}

for all
|\beta|\leq 2n
.

Next, one performs a priori estimates similar to those in §
5.2
,
6.2
: fix
T\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*}]
, a function
C_{t}:[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and a constant
\delta\geq 0
. Suppose that
A\geq 1
is a constant such that

(7.5a)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq A\mu_{m}

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n.
Suppose also that

(7.6a)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{K}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{k}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\mathring{Z}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{z}\varepsilon,

(7.6b)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{K}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{k}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}\widetilde{\mathring{Z}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{z}\varepsilon,

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n+1.
These hypotheses (
7.6
) allow us to apply the estimates of §
5.2
to our two solutions, the same way that (
6.4
) is used in §
6.2
. If
B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
and
C_{t}\gg(1+\alpha)3^{\delta}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, one obtains

(7.7)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+16A)\mu_{0},

(7.8)

\displaystyle\|\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq(3+2A)\mu_{0},

\displaystyle\|\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq(5+10A)\mu_{0}.

and

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 8\alpha A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\eta_{xt}-\widetilde{\eta}_{xt})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\leq 6(1+\alpha)A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{5}{8}A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 15A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\leq 5A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 17A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}\leq 7A\mu_{m},

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n
, as well as

(7.9)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m-1}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{10}{\alpha}A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m-1}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{5}{\alpha}A\mu_{m},

for all
t\in[0,T],\>1\leq m\leq 2n
.

Next, we use these a priori estimates to perform
L^{q}
energy estimates on the time derivatives of
(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})
and
(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})
, similar to those in Propositions
5.4
and
6.5
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Define the constant

(7.10)

B_{d}:=2\cdot 6^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}e^{31}.

Let
\delta
and
\kappa
satisfy (
5.10
) and let
C_{t}
be defined by (
5.11
). Let
\overline{C}_{t}
be the constant defined by (
5.27
). If
C_{t}(0)
satisfies

\displaystyle C_{t}(0)

\displaystyle\gg(1+\alpha)3^{\delta},

\displaystyle C_{t}(0)

\displaystyle\gg\max(1,\alpha)2^{\delta}C_{0},

and
\varepsilon
satisfies

\displaystyle(1+\alpha)(1+B_{z})\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ll 1,

\displaystyle 3^{\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}B_{d}(\overline{C}_{t}+1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ll 1,

then

(7.11a)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{d}\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{d}\mu_{m},

for all
t\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*})
and
0\leq m\leq 2n
.

The proof of this proposition is analogous to the proof of Proposition
5.4
and Proposition
6.5
. Fix
T\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*})
and pick a constant
A
satisfying
B_{d}<A<2B_{d}
. Our bootstrap hypothesis will be that

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq A\mu_{m},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq A\mu_{m},

for all
t\in[0,T],\>0\leq m\leq 2n.
Proposition
5.4
implies that (
7.6
) holds with the
\delta,\kappa
and
C_{t}
we have chosen. Therefore, all of the a priori estimates we have derived apply on
[0,T]
for this choice of
A
.

Just like in the proof of Proposition
5.4
, if we define
\mathcal{C}_{t}:=\frac{1}{2}2^{-\delta}C_{t}(0)
, then our hypotheses on
C_{t}(0)
allow us to apply our estimates at time
t=0
and get

(7.12)

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{3\mu_{m}}{2^{m}},

\displaystyle\frac{(m+1)^{2}\|\Sigma^{-\delta m}\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{m!C^{m}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{3\mu_{m}}{2^{m-1}},

for
m=0,\ldots,2n,t=0
. Therefore, our bootstrap hypothesis is true for
T=0
.

For
0\leq m\leq 2n
and
1<q<\infty
define the energies

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\eta_{x}|(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})|^{q}\>dx&m=0\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\delta qm}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})|^{q}\>dx&1\leq m\leq 2n\end{cases},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\frac{3}{\min(1,\alpha)}}\eta_{x}|(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})|^{q}\>dx&m=0\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\Sigma^{-\delta qm}\eta_{x}|\partial^{m}_{t}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})|^{q}\>dx&1\leq m\leq 2n\end{cases},

and for
1\leq m\leq 2n
,
1<q<\infty
, define the energies

\displaystyle\widehat{E}^{m,q}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(m+1)^{2q}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}}{(m!)^{q}C^{mq}_{t}},

\displaystyle\widehat{E}^{m,q}_{z}

\displaystyle:=\frac{(m+1)^{2q}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}}{(m!)^{q}C^{mq}_{t}}.

We prove (
7.11
) in the case
m=0
and
1\leq m\leq 2n
separately. In both cases, just like in the proofs of Propositions
5.4
,
6.5
, we take the time derivative of
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}
, simplify and rearrange using (
7.3a
) and (
7.3b
), and use (
4.8
) to produce damping terms which make
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{m,q}_{z}
satisfy

\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{k}+\dot{\widehat{\mathcal{E}}}^{0,q}_{z}\leq 15\max(1,\alpha)q(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{k}+\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{0,q}_{z})+2(A\mu_{0})^{q}\mathcal{O}(3^{\delta}(\overline{C}_{t}+1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}})^{q},

\displaystyle\dot{\widehat{E}}^{m,q}_{k}+\dot{\widehat{E}}^{m,q}_{z}\leq-mq\max(1,\alpha)(\widehat{E}^{m,q}_{k}+\widehat{E}^{m,q}_{z})+2(A\mu_{m})^{q}[m\mathcal{O}([\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}+(1+\alpha)B_{z}]\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}})^{q}+\mathcal{O}((1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}})^{q}].

From here the bootstrap argument closes by using ODE comparison and (
7.12
), provided that
\varepsilon
is small enough.

After proving Proposition
7.1
, one can deduce bounds on the rest of the derivatives of the difference via an induction argument, just like in Proposition
5.5
: if
\overline{C}_{x}
is a constant satisfying

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{x}

\displaystyle\gg 1,

\displaystyle\alpha\overline{C}_{x}

\displaystyle\gg\overline{C}_{t},

\varepsilon
satisfies
(1+\alpha)B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
, and
C_{t}(0)
satisfies
C_{t}(0)\gg 1
, then the following bounds hold for all
t\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*})
and
|\beta|\leq 2n
:

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\mathring{K}-\widetilde{\mathring{K}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\mathring{Z}-\widetilde{\mathring{Z}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 8\alpha B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 70B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\eta_{x}\mathring{W}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x}\widetilde{\mathring{W}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 7B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\Sigma-\widetilde{\Sigma})_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}\leq 120B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|},

Our efforts culminate in the following bounds:

(7.13a)

\displaystyle\frac{(|\beta|+1)^{2}\|\partial^{\beta}(\eta_{xt}-\widetilde{\eta}_{xt})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{|\beta|!\overline{C}^{\beta_{x}}_{x}\overline{C}^{\beta_{t}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha)38B_{d}\mu_{|\beta|}

for all
t\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*}),|\beta|\leq 2n
, and, as an immediate corollary,

(7.13b)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}(\eta_{x}-\widetilde{\eta}_{x})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}38(1+\alpha)t\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{d}\mu_{i}

for all
t\in[0,T_{*}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{*}),i=0,\ldots,2n
. We will use these bounds (
7.13
) in §
8.3
.

8.
Finite-codimension Banach manifolds of initial data

Recall that our aim (see Theorem
9.1
below) is to construct a codimension-
(2n-2)
Banach manifold of initial data
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\in(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
for which the unique classical solutions
(w,z,k)
to the corresponding Cauchy problem (
3.2
) form
C^{0,\frac{1}{2n+1}}
pre-shocks. In this section, we will show that the initial data
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})
in a particular neighborhood of
(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
for which the system

\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=0,\qquad\partial_{x}\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=0,\qquad\ldots\qquad\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=0,

has a solution
(x_{*},T_{*})
is a codimension-
(2n-2)
Banach manifold. In §
9
we will show that solutions
(w,z,k)
with initial data in this manifold form cusps resembling
-y^{\frac{1}{2n+1}}
at their first singularities.

8.1.
Assumptions on the initial data

Fix a positive integer
n
and a constant
C_{0}\geq 3
. Now fix a function
\overline{w}_{0}\in W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T})
satisfying

(8.1a)

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{5}{2}-x+\tfrac{x^{2n+1}}{2n+1},

\displaystyle|x-0|\leq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},

(8.1b)

\displaystyle\overline{w}^{\prime}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-1+C^{-2n}_{0},

\displaystyle|x-0|\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},

(8.1c)

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!}

\displaystyle\leq C^{i}_{0},

\displaystyle\forall\>i=0,\ldots,2n+1.

Note that

\displaystyle\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0}(x)=-\delta_{i0}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}i!{2n\choose{i}}x^{2n-i},\qquad|x-0|\leq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},

so that for
i=1,\ldots,2n+1
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(-\frac{1}{C_{0}},\frac{1}{C_{0}})}}{i!C^{i}_{0}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}{2n\choose{i}}C^{-2n}_{0}<1.

Therefore, our assumptions on
\overline{w}_{0}
are consistent.

With
\overline{w}_{0}
chosen as above, define

(8.2)

\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})\subset(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}

to be the set of all
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\in(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|w_{0}-\overline{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle<\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\|z_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle<\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}(w_{0}-\overline{w}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!}

\displaystyle<C^{i}_{0}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\forall\>i=0,\ldots,2n+1,

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}z_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!}

\displaystyle<C^{i}_{0}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\forall\>i=0,\ldots,2n+1,

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial^{i+1}_{x}k_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}}{i!}

\displaystyle<C^{i}_{0}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\forall\>i=0,\ldots,2n+1.

Since
\|\overline{w}_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=1
, we know that
\frac{3}{2}\leq\overline{w}_{0}\leq\frac{7}{2}
everywhere in
\mathbb{T}
. Therefore, if
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}
we have that
\frac{1}{2}<\sigma_{0}<2
so that
\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})\subset\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
. For the remainder of this paper, assume that
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}
so that this inclusion always holds.

8.2.
Preliminary Estimates along the Fast Acoustic Characteristics

For the remainder of this section, suppose that
\varepsilon,\overline{C}_{t},
and
\overline{C}_{x}
are chosen such that the conclusions of §
5
hold for all initial data
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\in\mathcal{A}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
. Furthermore, we will restrict our attention to only solutions with initial data
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})\in\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
.

Lemma 8.1
.

For all
i=0,\ldots,2n+1,t\in[0,T_{*})
, the following approximate identities are true:

(8.3a)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{xt}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(8.3b)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{x}-(\delta_{i0}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)t\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

As a result, for
|x-0|\leq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},t\in[0,T_{*}),i=0,\ldots,2n+1
we have

(8.4a)

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{xt}(x,t)-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\bigg[-\delta_{i0}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}i!{2n\choose{i}}x^{2n-i}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(8.4b)

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{x}(x,t)-\bigg[\delta_{i0}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t\bigg[-\delta_{i0}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}i!{2n\choose{i}}x^{2n-i}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)t\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

Proof.

The bounds (
8.3
) follow immediately from Corollary
5.7
, the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
, and the fact that
\overline{C}_{x}\geq e^{2}C_{0}
and
B_{z}>1
.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

If

C_{0}^{2n}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1,

then

(8.5)

\eta_{x}(x,t)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}C^{-2n}_{0}\qquad\forall\>|x-0|\geq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},t\in[0,T_{*}].

It follows from this that
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
must have at least one zero in
|x-0|<\frac{1}{C_{0}}
.

Proof.

For
|x-0|\geq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
, using (
8.3b
) with
i=0
, (
8.1
) , and (
4.12b
) gives us

\displaystyle\eta_{x}

\displaystyle\geq 1+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t[-1+C^{-2n}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)]

\displaystyle\geq 1+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}T_{*}[-1+C^{-2n}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)]

\displaystyle=1+(1+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon))[-1+C^{-2n}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)]=C^{-2n}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon).

Therefore, if
C^{2n}_{0}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
then we obtain (
8.5
).

Since
\min_{\mathbb{T}}\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})=0
,
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
must have a zero in
|x-0|<\frac{1}{C_{0}}
.
∎

Now, define the function
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}:\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

(8.6)

\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t):=\begin{cases}\eta_{x}(x,t)&t\leq T_{*}\\
\eta_{x}(x,T_{*})+(t-T_{*})\eta_{xt}(x,T_{*})&t\geq T_{*}\end{cases}.

\widetilde{\eta}_{x}
extends
\eta_{x}
to a
C^{2n,1}_{x}C^{1}_{t}
function on all of
\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)
in a manner analogous to the way that for Burgers equation the family of lines
1+tw^{\prime}_{0}(x)
extends
\eta_{x}
to all of
\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)
(see §
2
).

Lemma 8.3
.

For all
i=0,\ldots,2n,t\in[0,\infty)
, the following approximate identities are true:

(8.7a)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(8.7b)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}-(\delta_{i0}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\partial^{i+1}_{x}\overline{w}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)t\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

As a result, for
|x-0|\leq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}},t\geq 0,i=0,\ldots,2n
, we have that

(8.8a)

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t)-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\bigg[-\delta_{i0}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}i!{2n\choose{i}}x^{2n-i}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(8.8b)

\displaystyle\bigg|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)-\bigg[\delta_{i0}+\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t\bigg[-\delta_{i0}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{i\leq 2n\}}i!{2n\choose{i}}x^{2n-i}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 22\max(1,\alpha)t\tfrac{i!}{(i+1)^{2}}\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

In particular, if
B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
we obtain the bounds

(8.9)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{2}{3}(1+\alpha)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t)\leq-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}

\displaystyle\forall\>|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}.

Proof.

It is immediate from the definition of
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}
that

\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t)=\begin{cases}\eta_{xt}(x,t)&t\leq T_{*}\\
\eta_{xt}(x,T_{*})&t\geq T_{*}\end{cases}.

Taking
\partial^{i}_{x}
of
\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}
and applying (
8.3a
) and (
8.4a
) gives us (
8.7a
) and (
8.8a
) respectively.

Integrating (
8.7a
) and (
8.8a
) in time gives us (
8.7b
) and (
8.8b
) respectively. (
8.9
) is an immediate consequence of (
8.8a
) if
44B_{z}\varepsilon<\frac{1}{9}
.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

If
\overline{C}_{x}^{2n}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
, then for all
|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
and
t\geq\frac{2}{3}\frac{2}{1+\alpha}
, we have the lower bound

(8.10)

\partial^{2n}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)\geq\tfrac{(2n)!}{2}.

It follows that
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
can have at most
n
zeros in
\mathbb{T}
. In particular, when
n=1
,
\eta_{x}
always has a unique zero in
\mathbb{T}\times[0,T_{*}]
.

Proof.

Apply (
8.4b
) with
i=2n
to obtain

\partial^{2n}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}=\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t(2n)![1+\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}_{x}^{2n}B_{z}\varepsilon)]\geq\tfrac{2}{3}(2n)![1+\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}_{x}^{2n}B_{z}\varepsilon)].

If
\overline{C}_{x}^{2n}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough, the lower bound (
8.10
) follows.

It now follows from (
4.12a
) that
\partial^{2n}_{x}\eta_{x}(x,T_{*})\geq\frac{(2n)!}{2}
for all
|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
. Therefore
\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
is a strictly increasing function on the interval
[-\frac{1}{C_{0}},\frac{1}{C_{0}}]
, so it has no critical points and at most one zero in
[-\frac{1}{C_{0}},\frac{1}{C_{0}}]
.
This implies that
\partial^{2n-2}_{x}\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
has at most one critical point and at most two zeros in
[-\frac{1}{C_{0}},\frac{1}{C_{0}}]
. Continuing inductively in this manner,
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
has at most
2n-1
critical points and at most
2n
zeros in
[-\frac{1}{C_{0}},\frac{1}{C_{0}}]
. However, we know that
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})\geq 0
everywhere, so it follows from (
8.5
) that all zeros of
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
in
\mathbb{T}
must themselves be critical points in the interval
|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
. In between any two zeros of
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
must be at least one other critical point, so
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
can have at most
n
zeros.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

Suppose
(x_{*},t_{*})\in\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)
is a point such that
|x_{*}-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
and

(8.11)

\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x_{*},t_{*})=\partial_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x_{*},t_{*})=\ldots=\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x_{*},t_{*})=0.

Then
t_{*}=T_{*}
, and
x_{*}
is the unique zero of
\eta_{x}
in
\mathbb{T}\times[0,T_{*}]
. Furthermore,
x_{*}
satisfies the bound

(8.12a)

\displaystyle|x_{*}|^{2n}

\displaystyle\lesssim B_{z}\varepsilon.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x_{*},t_{*})=0
, we know
t_{*}\geq T_{*}
. Taylor expanding
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\cdot,t_{*})
about
\mathring{x}
and using (
8.10
) gives us

(8.13)

\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t_{*})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{*})^{2n},\qquad\forall|x-0|\leq\tfrac{1}{C_{0}}.

In particular,
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\cdot,t_{*})\geq 0
. It now follows from (
8.4a
) that

\displaystyle\eta_{x}(x,T_{*})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t_{*})-(t_{*}-T_{*})\eta_{xt}(x,T_{*})

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{*})^{2n}-(t_{*}-T_{*})\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\bigg[-1+x^{2n}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{*})^{2n}+(t_{*}-T_{*})\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\bigg[1-x^{2n}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{*})^{2n}+(t_{*}-T_{*})\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\bigg[1-C_{0}^{-2n}+\mathcal{O}(B_{z}\varepsilon)\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(x-x_{*})^{2n}+(t_{*}-T_{*})\tfrac{1+\alpha}{4}

for all
|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
. We know from Lemma
8.2
that
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
must have a zero and all zeros of
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
must be in the interval
\{|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}\}
. Since
t_{*}\geq T_{*}
, this is only possible if
t_{*}=T_{*}
and the zero must be
x=x_{*}
.

Since
t_{*}=T_{*}
, plugging
x=0
into (
8.13
) and using (
8.4b
) and (
4.12
) gives us

\displaystyle|x_{*}|^{2n}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\eta_{x}(0,T_{*})|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}T_{*}-1|+44\max(1,\alpha)T_{*}B_{z}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim B_{z}\varepsilon.

∎

Now define the function
G:\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

(8.14)

G(x,t):=\begin{bmatrix}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)\\
-\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)\end{bmatrix}.

Proposition 8.6
.

If

\displaystyle(1+\alpha)C_{0}<\overline{C}_{x},\qquad\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1,

then there exists a unique point
(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
in a ball of radius
\frac{1}{3(1+\alpha)C_{0}}
around the point
(0,\frac{2}{1+\alpha})
such that

G(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})=(0,0).

Proof.

For
|x-0|\leq\frac{1}{C_{0}}
, the formulas (
8.8
) give us

\displaystyle DG(x,t)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\partial^{2n}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)&\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t)\\
-\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}\partial_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t)&-\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t)\end{pmatrix}

(8.15)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t[1+\tfrac{\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon)}{(2n+1)^{2}}]&\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}[x+\frac{\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon)}{(2n)^{3}\overline{C}_{x}}]\\
t[-2nx^{2n-1}+\frac{\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon)}{\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}}]&1-x^{2n}+\tfrac{\mathcal{O}(\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon)}{\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}}\end{pmatrix}.

For
(x,t)
in a ball of radius
R:=\frac{1}{3(1+\alpha)C_{0}}
around
(0,\frac{2}{1+\alpha})
, the fact that
C_{0}\geq 3
implies that

\displaystyle|\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}t-1|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}R\leq\tfrac{1}{18},

\displaystyle 2n|x|^{2n-1}t

\displaystyle\leq 2nR^{2n-1}(\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}+R)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}|x|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}R\leq\tfrac{1}{18},

\displaystyle=12C_{0}n(R^{2})^{n}+2nR^{2n}

\displaystyle|x|^{2n}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{81(1+\alpha)^{2}},

\displaystyle\leq 12C_{0}R^{2}+2R^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{38}{81(1+\alpha)^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathrm{Id\,}-DG(x,t)\bigg|

\displaystyle<\tfrac{1}{2}+\mathcal{O}\big(\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon\big)

\displaystyle\forall\>(x,t)\in B_{R}((0,\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha})).

It follows that if
\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we have
\|\mathrm{Id\,}-DG\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}(B_{R}(0,\frac{2}{1+\alpha}))}\leq\frac{2}{3}
.

Plugging
(x,t)=(0,\frac{2}{1+\alpha})
into the formulas (
8.8
) yields

\displaystyle\big|\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(0,\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{44\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon}{(2n)^{3}\overline{C}_{x}},

\displaystyle\big|\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(0,\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha})\big|

\displaystyle\leq 88B_{z}\varepsilon.

Since
\overline{C}_{x}>(1+\alpha)C_{0}
, it follows that if
\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we have
|G(0,\frac{2}{1+\alpha})|<\frac{1}{3}R
. Our result now follows from Lemma
C.1
.
∎

Notice that one always has the inequality
\mathring{T}\geq T_{*}
: indeed, if
\>\mathring{T}\leq T_{*}
then

\eta_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})=\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})=0,

which means that
\mathring{T}=T_{*}
.

Corollary 8.7
.

If
(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
is the solution of
G=(0,0)
given by Proposition
8.6
, then
\mathring{x},\mathring{T}
satisfy the bounds

(8.16)

\displaystyle|\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\mathring{T}-1|

\displaystyle<\tfrac{1}{18},

(8.17)

\displaystyle|\mathring{x}|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{93}{(2n)^{3}}\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

It follows that if
n\geq 2
and
\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
then

(8.18a)

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}\tfrac{50}{(2n)}(2n)!\overline{C}^{2n-2}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

(8.18b)

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{53}{(2n)}(2n)!\overline{C}^{2n-2}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon,

for
1\leq i\leq 2n-2
.

Proof.

The inequality (
8.16
) is immediate from Proposition
8.6
and the fact that
C_{0}\geq 3
. Since
\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})=0
, (
8.10
) gives us

\displaystyle\big|\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(0,\mathring{T})\big|=\bigg|\int^{0}_{\mathring{x}}\partial^{2n}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,\mathring{T})\>dx\bigg|\geq\tfrac{(2n)!}{2}|\mathring{x}|.

Plugging
i=2n-1,x=0,t=\mathring{T}
, into (
8.8b
) now yields

\displaystyle|\mathring{x}|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{44}{(2n)^{3}}\max(1,\alpha)\mathring{T}\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon.

Our bound (
8.17
) now follows from (
8.16
).

Now suppose
n\geq 2
. Since
|\mathring{x}|<1
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq 2n-2}\tfrac{(2n)!}{(2n-i)!}|\mathring{x}|^{2n-i}=\tfrac{(2n)!}{2}|\mathring{x}|^{2}\leq\tfrac{(2n)!}{2}\big(\tfrac{93}{(2n)^{3}}\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon)^{2}.

For
1\leq i\leq 2n-2
the bound (
8.8a
) now implies

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(2n)!\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon\bigg[\tfrac{(93)^{2}}{2(2n)^{3}}\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon+44\tfrac{(2n-4)!}{(2n)!\overline{C}_{x}}\bigg].

Therefore, if
\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we get (
8.18a
). The bound (
8.18b
) follows from analogous computations using (
8.8b
).
∎

Now for
n\geq 2
define the function
f_{n}:\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2n-2},

(8.19)

f_{n}(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0}):=\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})\\
\partial^{2}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})\\
\vdots\\
\partial^{2n-2}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})\end{bmatrix}.

The zero set of
f_{n}
is precisely the initial data in
\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
for which the flow
\eta
forms an initial singularity of the form

(8.20)

\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=\partial_{x}\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=\ldots=\partial^{2n-1}_{x}\eta_{x}(x_{*},T_{*})=0,

at a point
x_{*}\in\mathbb{T}
, and whenever such a root
x_{*}
exists it must be the only root of
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
. To see this, if
f_{n}(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})=0
then it follows from the definitions of
G
and
(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
that
(x_{*},t_{*})=(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
solves (
8.11
), and Proposition
8.5
implies that
(x_{*},t_{*})=(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
is the unique root of
\eta_{x}
in
\mathbb{T}\times[0,T_{*}]
. Conversely, if
x_{*}\in\mathbb{T}
is a point solving (
8.20
) then Lemma
8.2
implies that
|x_{*}-0|<\frac{1}{C_{0}}
and Proposition
8.5
therefore implies that
x_{*}
is the only root of
\eta_{x}(\cdot,T_{*})
and also satisfies the bound (
8.12a
). Since we are assuming that
\overline{C}^{2n}_{x}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small and
(1+\alpha)C_{0}<\overline{C}_{x}
, as in the premise of Proposition
8.6
, it follows that
|x_{*}|<\frac{1}{3(1+\alpha)C_{0}}
and therefore
(x_{*},T_{*})=(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
.

We will now quantify the regularity of
f_{n}
so that we can apply the implicit function theorem and conclude that the zero set of
f_{n}
is the graph of a Lipschitz function from a codimension-
(2n-2)
subspace of
(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
into
\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}
.

8.3.
Stability of solutions

Now suppose further that
\overline{C}_{x},\overline{C}_{t}
are chosen large enough and
\varepsilon
is chosen small enough such that the hypotheses of §
7
are satisfied, and therefore the bounds (
7.13
) apply for all initial data in
\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
.

Proposition 8.8
.

The blowup time,
T_{*}
, and the extensions
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}
depend on the initial data in way that is Lipschitz with respect to the
W^{2n+2,\infty}
norm on
\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
: i.e. if
\|\cdot\|_{m,C_{0}}
is the seminorm on
(W^{2n+2,\infty}(\mathbb{T}))^{3}
defined by (
7.1
) and
(w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0}),(w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})\in\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0})
we have

(8.21)

\displaystyle|T_{*}(w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-T_{*}(w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{140}{1+\alpha}B_{d}\|(w^{1}_{0}-w^{2}_{0},z^{1}_{0}-z^{2}_{0},k^{1}_{0}-k^{2}_{0})\|_{0,C_{0}},

and if
\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon\ll 1
then for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}\times[0,\infty)
,
i=0,\ldots,2n-1
we have

(8.22a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq 45\frac{(1+\alpha)i!\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{d}}{i+1}\|(w^{1}_{0}-w^{2}_{0},z^{1}_{0}-z^{2}_{0},k^{1}_{0}-k^{2}_{0})\|_{i,C_{0}},

(8.22b)

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t,w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(x,t,w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq 45\frac{(1+\alpha)ti!\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{d}}{i+1}\|(w^{1}_{0}-w^{2}_{0},z^{1}_{0}-z^{2}_{0},k^{1}_{0}-k^{2}_{0})\|_{i,C_{0}}.

Here
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}(\cdot,\cdot,w^{j}_{0},z^{j}_{0},k^{j}_{0})
is the function
\widetilde{\eta}_{x}
corresponding to the solution with initial data
(w^{j}_{0},z^{j}_{0},k^{j}_{0})
.

Proof.

Let us adopt the notation
T^{j}_{*}:=T_{*}(w^{j}_{0},z^{j}_{0},k^{j}_{0})
for
j=1,2
and
\mu_{i}:=\|(w^{1}_{0}-w^{2}_{0},z^{1}_{0}-z^{2}_{0},k^{1}_{0}-k^{2}_{0})\|_{i,C_{0}}
. Suppose without loss of generality that
T^{1}_{*}\leq T^{2}_{*}
. There exists a point
x^{1}_{*}\in\mathbb{T}
with
|x^{1}_{*}-0|<\frac{1}{C_{0}}
such that

\eta_{x}(x^{1}_{*},T^{1}_{*},w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})=0.

Since
T^{2}_{*}\geq T^{1}_{*}
, it now follows from (
7.13
) and (
8.9
) that

0\leq\eta_{x}(x^{1}_{*},T^{2}_{*},w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})\leq 70B_{d}\mu_{0}-\tfrac{1+\alpha}{2}(T^{2}_{*}-T^{1}_{*}).

The inequality (
8.21
) now follows immediately.

For
t\leq T^{1}_{*}
, Corollary
7.13
implies

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq 38\frac{(1+\alpha)i!\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{d}}{(i+1)^{2}}\mu_{i}

for all
x\in\mathbb{T}
. For
T^{1}_{*}\leq t
, (
5.32c
) gives us

\displaystyle|\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-\partial^{i}_{x}\widetilde{\eta}_{xt}(x,t,w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial^{i}_{x}\partial_{t}\eta_{xt}(w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,t}}(t\wedge T^{2}_{*}-T^{1}_{*})+38\tfrac{(1+\alpha)i!\overline{C}^{i}_{x}B_{d}}{(i+1)^{2}}\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+\alpha)\overline{C}^{i}_{x}i!}{i+1}\big[21\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon(T^{2}_{*}-T^{1}_{*})+38\tfrac{B_{d}\mu_{i}}{i+1}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+\alpha)\overline{C}^{i}_{x}i!B_{d}}{i+1}\mu_{i}\big[21\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon\cdot\tfrac{140}{1+\alpha}\tfrac{\mu_{0}}{\mu_{i}}+38\big].

Therefore, if
\overline{C}_{t}B_{z}\varepsilon
is small enough we obtain (
8.22a
). The inequality (
8.22b
) now follows from using the fact that
\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{x}
is always constant at time
t=0
with
\partial^{i}_{x}\eta_{x}(x,0)=\delta_{i0}
and integrating (
8.22a
) in time.
∎

One immediate consequence of Proposition
8.8
is that the function
G
defined in (
8.14
) is Lipschitz in
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})
with

\displaystyle|G(x,t,w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0})-G(x,t,w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq 135B_{d}\big[\tfrac{\overline{C}^{2n-1}_{x}}{(2n)^{2}}+\tfrac{2}{1+\alpha}\big]\|(w^{1}_{0}-w^{2}_{0},z^{1}_{0}-z^{2}_{0},k^{1}_{0}-k^{2}_{0})\|_{2n-1,C_{0}}

for all
(x,t)\in\mathbb{T}\times[0,\frac{3}{1+\alpha}]
,
(w^{1}_{0},z^{1}_{0},k^{1}_{0}),(w^{2}_{0},z^{2}_{0},k^{2}_{0})\in\mathcal{B}_{n}(\varepsilon,C_{0}).
As a result (see §
C
),
(\mathring{x},\mathring{T})
is a Lipschitz function of
(w_{0},z_{0},k_{0})
with
